I am writing in response to your letter dated 19 June regarding the release of the Ministry of
Defence report entitled “Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region” and your
comments about your own UFO sightings.

As you will be aware from previous correspondence, the MOD does not have any expertise or role
in respect of 'UFO/flying saucer’ matters or to the question of the existence or otherwise of
extraterrestrial lifeforms. We remain totally open-minded, but to date the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena. We do not doubt that
individuals observe phenomenon that they are unable to identify, but believe that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them. It is not,
however, the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could
not justify expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence
remit.

With regard to your comments about the content of the Ministry of Defence UAP report, I can
advise you that the study was conducted purely to establish whether the UFO sighting reports
received by the MOD were of any value to the Defence Intelligence Staff (DIS) and whether there
was a requirement for the DIS to see them in the future. Given the conclusion it was decided that
there was no such requirement and, since December 2000, UFO reports have not been forwarded
to the DIS. We appreciate that there may be those who disagree with the report’s findings, but
the MOD is satisfied that its conclusions are sufficient for defence needs.

Yours sincerely,

Netley Abbey, ; Trent nee nannaes

Sir; S

I have been browsing here and there in your much publicised 400 page

“Freedom of Information” and feel impelled to comment, after MANY years of

actual involvement one to one...with what we still call “UFO"”s

although I now feel that “The Visitors” would be a more apt
description.

In March 1960 1 was a humble insurance agent on my local Gosport,
Hants, “debit”,and crossing a railway bridge in the town. My left eye

was caught by a glint in the bright, clear, cold, and windy sky.1 looked

at it and saw a “cigar” shaped object apparently hovering at about a
thousand feet. I looked hard at it and decided it was an aircraft

fighting a very strong wind from the north east.A young man passed by,
pushing his bike. I asked him if he thought the object was a “plane. He looked,
and said yes,it WAS a ‘plane. Satisfied,I walked on. The word “flying
saucer” has flicked across my mind but was dismissed. I'd read about such
in the press etc,,had an open mind about it. Five years in the wartime

RAF had given me much food for thought subsequently. Why not? I

had been, still am, an “expert” on aircraft recognition. Prewar I was

in the AIR TRAINING CORP and stationed on an aircraft Factory

roof at Fareham, where I worked at 16,as its “spotter”, during the early
war years, I pressed the buzzer and got some 100 workers into our shelter
as the sound and then the brief glimpse of two Junkers JU 88s breaking
low cloud was followed by bomb explosions and then AA batteries...too
late! HMS COLLINGWOOD was a huge naval training base just a mile
or so away. We learnt, later that a number of Wrens etc had been lining up
for their Friday payday. Identification had been too late. Not unusual
confusion with our own Blenhiem Bombers. The death toll was appalling.
I actually lived just a few hundred yards away from the establishment.

Relevant on that 1960 March morning? As I heard the roar of jet engines
thirty minutes after my initial sighting while talking to a client I ran to

a vantage point as two Meteor jets hove into view low over the rooftops,
heading up where, to my amazement, the “plane” was still hovering. I
KNEW then it was no aircraft! Those jets had been “scrambled” from a
nearby airbase. As the aircraft drew near the object, it lazily turned on end
in the clear blue sky and vanished! I ran into a nearby phone box. Found
the number for Thorney Island Airbase,got the control tower officer, gave
him my name and address, asked him what was the object those two Meteor
jets were chasing?

His answer changed my life, at 30 odd
years of age. “You saw no object, you

saw no jets’ I tried again. Precisely the same answer. I left that phone box
my mind in a whirl. Cold War Russian surveillance aircraft? it had been over
a very sensitive Admiralty Air Surface Weapons Establishment. (ASWE)

Next day my then brother in law called in on a social visit. Coincidence?
Much later in life I LEARNT about “coincidences”> Jung called them
“synchronicities”.

My wife went out to the kitchen to make a cup of tea for our visitor. WHO
WORKED AT ASWE! Indulging in “small talk” I said; * You had some
excitement over your place yesterday morning’ His face went grey.Then
WHITE.His mouth set in a grimline. He never spoke. NEVER DID!

I changed the subject.

On subsequent visits over the years he never brought the subject up as my
“library” of “flying Saucer” books grew and even when I appeared on
Southern TV speaking on “flying saucers”.

So,you see,all is relevant. All is “connected”. 1 mean, WHO did I go to
in authority after that on the spot refusal? But IKNEW! For ever! To

this day.The photographic proof lies in your hands. YOU don’t want it.
YOU KNOW. But SCARED TO TELL US...the PEOPLE,THE WORLD.

My 400 page manuscript lies in my dusty bookcase. Another 400 pages
will say “THE END”.Or WILL IT? That 1960 incident appeared in top
author Nick Redfern’s splendid FACTUAL best seller in 1997 under
“4 COVERT AGENDA”....FACTS from the OFFICIAL REPORTS.

He has written others. None equal that first one. He wrote in it at a book
presentation; ‘To Ernie.Hope you enjoy the book and thanks for your help

MY HELP? Of course, why not? He was telling us.me, there were many
others having the same experiences. He put me on to his publisher.

No joy there. You see, by then,1997, I “d had MORE sightings..afresh in
1977 and 1978 One with other witnesses five miles away. Another that

I didn’t recognise with my then wife in 1978...also with a close by neighbour
whose story appeared in my local Southampton Daily Echo. The last of
THREE in that January 1978 period I was given a swift trip around the

Solar system! Funny, I thought! Odd too. I started my own small UFO
Group...two ladies and myself.Applicants HAD TOHAVE HAD ACTUAL
EXPERIENCES. Together we had many more, even stranger. Odd dreams,
I “became” a “healer.. We had “spiritual” experiences. “Sittings” and “spiritual”
visits to church or churches produced more awesome

incidents. I spoke in public,on radio, TV, on such. Read books that equalled
in content what I was going through... AFTER I'd had them.

To sum up what is an ongoing and LONG LONG LONG story. Although
I believe the end, the conclusion,is not far off. Why?How?
Cast your eye over a selection of photos. Remember our UFO group has
had videos shown to the MOD top man Sir Lewis Mooney a few years
back,made into a very good BBC HORIZON “Paranormal” series, featured
our local Police Spotter plane encircling (twice) a strange object hovering
and moving over Southampton Water in 2001 and 2002 with dates almost
to the same day. Totally denied first of all by ALL local authorities.
Eventually confirmed by Police Spotter unit,times,locations,confirmed.
However, the crews ‘saw nothing’. ‘Possibly Jupiter.. Which I thought
was pretty good technology for a humble spotter plane,and said so.

Later certain matters confirmed the TRUTH as we knew and filmed it.

‘Course,you may not KNOW all this. Then again.................::0 ?
OK Had enough?

T'll just leave you to ponder. Much as the total SILENCE from the media
...even from dear old Nick Pope whose first wad of photos earlier this
year he thought “fascinating” but the very recent ones...particularly

the “humanoid” has brought WEEKS OF NO REACTION. I suggested
‘awesome’ might be more apt this time.

Sincerely

Public Relations Officer
Southampton UFO and PARANORMAL GROUP (SUFOG)
1995

UFO Spotting

The Scuthampion UFO Group
have been investigating the
unexplained, the mysterious and
the potentially ‘out of this world’
objects spotted in the skies
above Hampshire aver the past
15 years.

Members of the Southampton UFO Group regularly scour the skies
looking for any unidentified objects which have no explanation, and
just might be alien craft,

Reynolds.

Ernie's experiences of unidentified objects go back to 1960 when he
was in Gosport and saw an object in daylight above Portsdown Hill:
“This object was hovering and shining in the March sun. I just
thought it was an airplane but half an hour later I heard the roar of
jet engines when two Meteor jets were scrambled towards this
object.

The jets got near it and it jazily turned
on end and disappeared, and the jets
went round in circles. I wenttoacall 4

That wasn't the only time when Ernie's life was changed by forces

investigations

Steve Gerard is the founder of the Southampton UFO Group. They
research and investigate any reported unusual sightings in the local
skies, contact relevant agencies, military, airports, etc and check star
charts to find an explanation for objects or lights in the sky: "In 95-
6% of cases it turns out to have a rational explanation - if we come
up with a dead end, we class it as a UFO - it doesn't necessarily
mean it comes from ‘out there’ as it were, just that we can't explain
it."

yo BBC - Hampshire - Features - UFO Spotting Page 2 of 3

fy

"We've had a rash of reports in the Pompey area with photographs
and video footage." says Steve, but military activity or even lights or
lazers from nightclubs or entertainment venues can also cause
mistaken spottings. “It may not be what people want to hear but
people don’t know what they are looking at. A lot of time they could
be looking at mundane objects, or lights in the sky."

"We do get an upsurge of interest follow a TV programme -
sometimes people's minds are ted and perceive they've seen
something similar to what they've seen on telly."

Out of this World

But what about alien abductions? - "We've had cases of abductions
and experiments on people who come back with marks and scratches
on themselves - it's fairly rare, but it does happen - not as often as
the UFO sightings."

The daily life of a UFO spotter involves spending long hours at night
scouring the skies for activity, as Steve explains: “At 3 in the
morning at minus four at the top of Cheesefoot Head, you've got to
be quite dedicated! It's surprising if you've got a good interest the
hours just go by. I've got an interest in the stars and space - we
can't be the only life form out there, so just watching the stars, you
feel you're somewhere different."

And will we ever get conclusive proof that aliens have visited
Hampshire? - Steve is sceptical: "It won't be in my lifetime and, if we
did, I'd be surprised. But if we're watching them and they're
watching us...”

Disappeared

Wartime airfields now listed

In a lighter frame of mind

WARTIME airfields linked to the Battle of Britain and the
Dambusier raids are to become listed sites, writes
Maggie Wilson.

This means they're protected from property developers and
will be looked after, as they can’t be demolished without
consent.

The new protection status has been granted to a total of
255 buildings and sites connected to both World Wars, and
were identified through a survey of military aviation sites by
English Heritage.

The move means thai Britain's first-ever airfield containing
the World War | hangars at Larkhall, Wiltshire, will become a
historic aviaiion site.

Scampion bomber station, where 617 Squadron of the
Darmbusiter raids was stationed, is also protected.

The few to whom we owe so much will be remembered by
ihe preservation of Battle of Britain fighter stations at Debden,
Essex and Dixford, Cambridgeshire.

8 1938 bunker which formed the operations HQ for the

f Britain receives Grade 4 status, meaning that it’s of
special interest, Of ihe 500,000 listed buildings in England and
Wales, only two per cent have achieved this status.

“These and the buildings on them are testimony to
i of heroes,” said Culture Minister David Lammy.
riant we re ber and learn from the past.
lopefully, tha prolection of these historic sites will help to
ure that.”

REVELLERS in a town centre are being lulled into
good humour on their way home from the bars and
clubs with the introduction of mood lighting, writes
Hazel Crossan.

The North Wales town of Wrexham has introduced the
idea to calm people down, in the hope they send them
homeward in a more mellow frame of mind when they spill
out of the various nightspots in the area in the early hours
of the morning.

The low-level lights, set into the pavement, slowly
change from blue to green, then to red, all in a 10-minute
time frame,

“The whole area has been undergoing a regeneration
programme over the last few years, and the lights are reaily
striking when you see them operate alongside our stainless
steel street furniture,” said Urban Development Manager
isabel Waison.

“You're noi really aware of them changing then,
suddenly. they're a different colour.

“We've had sonie very positive feedback from a wide
variety of peoxie, including the police.”

it’s long been accepted that colour can have an effect on
mood, and it’s heped that clubbers will wend their way
home in a happier frame of mind, reducing aggression and
thorefore anti-social behaviour, thanks to the innovative

t

Beeline to a familiar face

SCIENTISTS have proved that, despite
their tiny brains, bees can be trained to ~~:
recognise faces, writes Hazel Crossan.

in a series of experiments, an international ““"-
team of scientists led by Dr Adrian Dyer of
Cambridge University introduced bees to a s
selection of black-and-white photographs of human
faces. ee
Tiny containers beneath them contained either sugar or
bitter quinine.

The bees soon learned to land on the platform under the
face where they got the sweet reward — and just to make
things more difficult, the photographs were moved around to
make sure they were honing In on the photograph, rather than
where it was placed on a board.

Once the sugar was removed, the insects stil] made a
beeline for the faces that had rewarded them, and ignored the
others.

“The results in this study show that bees are capable of
recognising human faces, despite having less than 0.1 per
cent of the number of neurons (nerve cells) found in the
human brain,” said Dr Dyer.

“Further testing showed that bees formed a long-term
memory and were still able to recognise the target face two
days faier.”

It's hoped the research could help with conditions like

autism where some people have difficulty in recognising faces. --

Pea section 40

I am writing with reference to your letter and newspaper articles regarding the release of the MOD
report entitled ‘Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region’, that I received on
the 14 May 2006. This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence
regarding UFOs (also known as UAP) and the Department jointly responsible with the Defence
Intelligence Staff (DIS) for the release of this report. I apologise for the long delay in replying.

The Ministry of Defence understands the public interest in information about UFOs and has
embraced the spirit of open government. All requests for information are answered as fully as
possible and, despite the size and original classification of this report, staff have taken great care
to ensure that the majority of this large report is made available. We are pleased to be able to
make the report available to a wide audience via the MOD website and if you have not yet had an
opportunity to see the full report, you may wish to view it at

http://www.mod.uk/DefenceInternet/FreedomO finformation/PublicationScheme.

With regard to your comments about the report, I can advise you that the study was conducted
purely to establish whether the UFO sighting reports received by the MOD were of any value to
the DIS and whether there was a requirement for the DIS to see them in the future. Given the
conclusion it was decided that there was no such requirement and, since December 2000, UFO
reports have not been forwarded to the DIS. We appreciate that there may be those who disagree
with the report’s findings, but the MOD is satisfied that it’s conclusions are sufficient for defence
needs.

Finally, the details of your UFO sightings have been noted and your letter and copies of the
newspaper articles about the study will be placed on our files.

Yours sincerely

—
Shipston.On Stour
Warwickshire ao

Deo

I am writing with reference to your letter dated 21" June 2006. Your letter has been passed to this
Department as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating
to ‘UFOs.’ Sorry for the delay in replying.

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
| ‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
| defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to date no ‘UFO’ report has
| revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported

to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be
found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to
| provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence

resources if we were to do so.

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters to the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

Finally, you may be interested to know that there is already some information about UFOs
available for public viewing. MOD files were destroyed for 5 years until 1967 when they were
generally preserved for the National Archives. A few have survived before 1967 and these
together with records up to 1977 are now available for viewing. The National Archives can be
contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Kew, Richmond, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone, 020 8876 3444. If
you have access to the internet, The National Archives have a website containing an on-line
catalogue of the records they hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet at
http://www.nationalarchives.gov.uk. Also, the Ministry of Defence Freedom of Information
Scheme also contains documents relating to well known events in Rendlesham Forest, Suffolk in
1980 and the final report of the Flying Saucer Working Party in 1951. These can be accessed via
®@ the internet at http://www.mod.uk. Further information can also be found in the Publication
Scheme Disclosure Log.

Thope this is helpful.

Yours sincerely

&

1 am writing with reference to your e.mail back to me - dated 15 June 2006.

You mentioned about the issue of defence in your e.mail. Well, | can assure you that the UK’s
airspace in peacetime is maintained through continuous surveillance of the UK Air Policing
Area by the Royal Air Force. This is achieved by using a combination of civil and military
radar installations, which provide a continuous real-time ‘picture’ of the UK airspace. Any
threat to the UK Air Defence Region would be handled in the light of the particular
circumstances at the time (it might if deemed appropriate, involve the scrambling of diversion
of air defence aircraft). This applies to any threat to the UK’s security from whatever source. |
should point out that to date, the MOD is not aware of any evidence which substantiate the
existence of craft or life forms of extraterrestrial origin, and no threat has been discerned
which has been attributed to a ‘UFO’.

Finally, the MOD does not rely on members of the public to inform us of sightings for defence
reasons, we have a strategy in place, (as mentioned above). Therefore, reports are not
investigated, and we do not have the remit to investigate aerial phenomena, but we do remain
vigilant for anything which might indicate that the integrity of the UK’s Air Defence Region
may have been compromised.

| hope that puts your mind at rest and is helpful.

Yours sincerely

Ministry of Defence

Dear Sir

I am writing with reference to your report of an ‘unidentified flying
object', that you saw above your home, the details of which were
passed to this Department. This office is the focal point within the
Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs'.

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence
examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying objects' it receives
solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United
Kingdom's airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air
activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom
from an external source, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such
evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each
sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such
as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them if
resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function
of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It
would be an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

VVVVVVVV VV VV VV VY

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of 'UFO/flying
saucer' matters to the question of the existence or otherwise of
extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally open-minded.
I should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

VVVVVVVVV VV VV VV VY

>

On regard to your particular observation, what you saw could have
been due to remnants of a fire that was in a Manchester Warehouse,
although you haven't given this office a date or time of your
sighting, to see if the dates match up, but then that is only a
theory. Also, I can't confirm whether we received any other 'UFO'

I am writing concerning your letter dated 20" May regarding an article in the Auckland Herald.
-I assume this article referred to the release of a UK Ministry of Defence report entitled
“Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region”. Your letter has been passed to
this Department as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence
regarding UFOs (also known as UAP) and the Department jointly responsible with the Defence
Intelligence Staff (DIS) for the release of this report.

First it may be helpful if I explain that the MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
'UFO/flying saucer’ matters or to the question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial
lifeforms. We remain totally open-minded, but to date the MOD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena. The MOD examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of
a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such
as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of
public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

With regard to the released report, I can advise you that the MOD understands the public interest
in information about UFOs and has embraced the spirit of open government. All requests for
information are answered as fully as possible and, despite the size and original classification of
this report, staff have taken great care to ensure that the majority of this large report is made
available, We are pleased to be able to make the report available to a wide audience via the MOD
website and if you have not yet had an opportunity to see the full report, you may wish to view it

at http://www.mod.uk/Defencelnternet/FreedomOfinformation/PublicationScheme .

As for your comments about the content of the report, I can advise you that the study was
conducted purely to establish whether the UFO sighting reports received by the MOD were of any
value to the Defence Intelligence Staff and whether there was a requirement for the DIS to see
them in the future. Given the conclusion it was decided that there was no such requirement and,
since December 2000, UFO reports have not been forwarded to the DIS. We appreciate that there

may be those who disagree with the report’s findings, but the MOD is satisfied that its
conclusions are sufficient for defence needs.

Your sincerely,

The Prime Minister/SofS/Min(AF)/Min(DPYU ‘SofS/MOD" has received the attached
correspondence from a member of the public, which this office has neither retained nor
acknowledged. Please send a reply on behalf of the PM/Minister/Department .

Ministers attach great importance to correspondence being answered promptly, and your reply

_ should be sent within 15 working days of the above date. If, exceptionally, this should prove
impossible, an interim reply should be sent within the same timescale. You should be aware that
No 10 periodically calls for a sample of letters sent by officials on the PM's behalf for his
perusal. ;

Most correspondence involves some form of request for information — even if it is only a request
for clarification of Government policy —and is therefore covered by the Freedom of Information
Act (FOIA) from January 2005. In general, if you meet the deadline for responding to
correspondence, and comply with any requests for information, there is no need to do anything
differently as this will meet the requirements of the Act. However, if the correspondence
requests information which is not already in the public domain, and which might need to be
withheld, then you should treat it as a FOIA request, track it using the Access to Information
toolkit, and comply with the separate FOI guidance from DG Info (see
http://aitportal/default.aspx for details). However, the deadline for responding to correspondence
will still apply. If you are in any doubt as to whether a piece of correspondence should be treated
as an FOIA request, you should ask your FOI Focal Point or refer to the guidance produced by
DG Info. :

records on correspondence will be performed throughout the year.

Bradford 13 June 2006
West Yorkshire

I am writing concerning your letter in response to my letter of 17" May 2006.

The “basic package” you describe is not distributed by the Ministry of Defence. As described in
my previous letter, once we have established that there is no evidence that the United Kingdom's
airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity, we do not attempt
to positively identify what was seen. We do not therefore have records of “identified” and
“unidentified” sighting reports. You also say that in 1995 there were 142 sightings reported. The
MOD received 373 sighting reports that year, so clearly this information did not come from the
MOD.

You also mention frequent reports of commercial and military aircraft being “buzzed” by UFOs
as reported in Paranormal Magazine. The MOD receives only a few such report each year and
there is no evidence that these are becoming more frequent. Any reports from pilots or aircrew
(civilian or military) are referred to air defence experts within the department, but none have
revealed any evidence of a threat to the integrity of the UK’s airspace.

You asked why the MOD has a department that “investigates” individual UFO sighting reports
from the public, if funds cannot be justified to research the potential threat posed by
extraterrestrial craft. It may be helpful if I clarify that the MOD does not “investigate” UFO
sightings. The integrity of the UK's airspace in peacetime is maintained through continuous
surveillance of the UK Air Policing Area by the Royal Air Force. This is achieved by using a
combination of civil and military radar installations, which provide a continuous real-time
“picture” of the UK airspace. Any threat to the UK Air Policing Area would be handled in the
light of the particular circumstances at the time (it might if deemed appropriate, involve the
scrambling or diversion of air defence aircraft). From that perspective, reports provided to us of
‘UFO’ sightings are examined, but consultation with air defence staff and others as necessary is
considered only where there is sufficient evidence to suggest a breach of UK air space. The vast
majority of reports we receive are very sketchy and vague. Only a handful of reports in recent
years have warranted further examination and none revealed any evidence of a threat.

With regard to your remarks about surveillance of internet sites, ] can inform you that the MOD
has no interest in sites viewed by those with an interest in UFOs and does not carry out any such
activity.

’

Finally, you. comment about MOD files being incomplete and inaccurate because we have been

que to supply information relating to “the greys” or the “Mihame’’. It is not a question of

accuracy. The MOD know of no evidence that substantiates the existence of such phenomena.
The only relevant records we might hold is if members of the public have written to the MOD
about these. We currently hold UFO sighting reports and correspondence records spanning a 25
year period. Clearly, if we were to search all these records the costs would quickly exceed the
permitted £600 limit set for compliance with the Freedom of Information Act 2000 and as
provided by Section 12 of the Act, the Ministry of Defence is not obliged to carry out such an
exercise.

Thope this is helpful.

Yours sincerely,

de

Tam writing concerning your letter of 24" May addressed to Section 40 | regarding UFOs. This
department is the focal m0 within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence regarding UFO

matters and I am successor.
It may assist you if I explain that the MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
‘UFO/flying saucer' matters or to the question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial
lifeforms. We remain totally open-minded, but to date the MOD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena. Any reports of 'UFOs' the MOD receives
are examined solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance;
namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace might have been
compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a potential threat
to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such
evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it
is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft li ghts or natural phenomena, could be found
for them, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service.
We could not justify expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific
defence remit.

If you would like to see some of the information on UFOs released by the MOD please look at the
Freedom of Information pages of the MOD website at :
http://www.mod.uk/Defencelnternet/FreedomOfinformation. There are also MOD UFO files
from the 1950s, 1960s and early 1970s open for viewing at The National Archives, Ruskin
Avenue, Kew, Richmond, Surrey. Details of how to access these records and an online catalogue
can be found on The National Archives website at http://www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

T hope this is helpful.

Yours si

pre cection 4g

T am writing concerning your letter dated 18 May and your email of 22 May regarding further
comments on the release of the MOD report entitled “Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK
Air Defence Region”.

I am sorry to hear you seem to have had difficulties navigating the documents on the MOD
website. As the report is very large, it has been split in to a number of pdf files to make it easy
and quick to download. In order to make the report easy to find, it has been highlighted in the Top
Choices section on the Freedom of Information home page, and a direct link has been provided to
all the volumes in the Publication Scheme. Links have also been provided between the letter to
the applicant who made the original Freedom of Information request which is in the Disclosure
Log Top Choices and the documents in the Publication Scheme. You should also be able to
navigate effectively between the Volumes.

With regard to your remarks about the use of “spiders”, I can assure you that the MOD has
simply made these documents available on our website and has no reason to engage in this type of
activity.

You also made some comments about the contents of the report and your theory that this report
was released as propaganda to “counter the gathering momentum for disclosure”. We appreciate
that there may be those who disagree with the report’s findings, but the MOD is satisfied that its
conclusions are sufficient for defence needs. The circumstances surrounding the release of the
report has already been explained in my previous letter. As for the disclosure of information in
general, anyone (anywhere in the World) is able to make a Freedom of Information Act request
and if the MOD holds relevant information and we are able to disclose it, we will. A great deal of
information has already been released under the Freedom of Information Act and prior to the
introduction of the Act, in accordance with the Code of Practice on Access to Government
Information. In addition there is information open for public viewing at The National Archives,
Ruskin Avenue, Kew, Richmond, Surrey. Further details can be found on their website at

http://www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely,

London

premsection 40 |

I am writing with reference to your recent message left on Das answerphone. This office is the
focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to ‘UFOs’.

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to date no ‘UFO’ report has
revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be
found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to
provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence
resources if we were to do so.

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters to the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

With regard to your sightings, they have been noted.
Finally, I should like to assure you that Defence technology, including the effectiveness of our air

defence systems, is constantly evolving and we are confident that our air defence capabilities fully
meet the air defence threat and protect the integrity of the UK Air Defence Region.

Yours sincerely

H

I am writing in response to your letter concerning ‘unidentified flying objects’ seen over Glasgow.
Your letter has been passed to this department as we are the focal point within the Ministry of
Defence for correspondence about ‘UFOs’. I apologise for the delay in replying.

First it may be helpful if I explain that the MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
'UFO/flying saucer’ matters or to the question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial
lifeforms. We remain totally open-minded, but to date the MOD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena. The MOD examines any reports of
'UFOs' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace might
have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such
as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of
public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit

With regard to your comments that the defence systems of this country is being breached, I can
assure you that the integrity of the UK's airspace in peacetime is maintained through continuous
surveillance of the UK Air Policing Area by the Royal Air Force. This is achieved by using a
combination of civil and military radar installations, which provide a continuous real-time
“picture” of the UK airspace. Any threat to the UK Air Policing Area would be handled in the
light of the particular circumstances at the time (it might if deemed appropriate, involve the
scrambling or diversion of air defence aircraft). From that perspective, reports provided to us of
“UFO” sightings are examined, but consultation with air defence staff and others as necessary is
considered only where there is sufficient evidence to suggest a breach of UK air space. The vast
majority of reports we receive are very sketchy and vague. Only a handful of reports in recent
years have warranted further investigation and none revealed any evidence of a threat.

Ihave viewed your video and am content that it contains nothing of defence concern. As
requested it is hereby returned to you.

Yours sincerely,

The Prime Minister/SofS/Min(AFY/Min(DP)/USofS/MOD* has received the attached
correspondence from a member of the public, which this office has neither retained nor
acknowledged. Please send a reply on behalf of the PM/Minister/Department’.

Ministers attach great importance to correspondence being answered promptly, and your reply
should be sent within 15 working days of the above date. If, exceptionally, this should prove
impossible, an interim reply should be sent within the same timescale. You should be aware that

> No 10 periodically calls for a sample of letters sent by officials on the PM's behalf for his

perusal.

Most correspondence involves some form of request for information ~ even if it is only a request
for clarification of Government policy ~ and is therefore coveréd by the Freedom of Information
Act (FOIA) from January 2005. In general, if you meet the deadline for responding to
correspondence, and comply with any requests for information, there is no need to do anything
differently as this will meet the requirements of the Act. However, if the correspondence
requests information which is not already in the public domain, and which might need to be
withheld, then you should treat it as a FOIA request, track it using the Access to Information
toolkit, and comply with the separate FOI guidance from DG Info (see
http://aitportal/default.aspx for details). However, the deadline for responding to correspondence
will still apply. If you are in any doubt as to whether a piece of correspondence should be treated
as an FOIA request, you should ask your FOI Focal Point or refer to the guidance produced by
DG Info. :

It is vital that branches ensure they have simple systems to record and track correspondence
received from members of the public. This information should be regularly monitored and
reviewed against the targets for answering correspondence published in the Spending Review

2000 Service Delivery Agreement for the Ministry of Defence.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the accuracy of your branch
records on correspondence will be performed throughout the year.

Abingdon D/DAS/64/3
Northampton Date:

6 June 2006

I am writing with reference to your message left on Das answerphone on 5 June 2006. This office
is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to ‘UFOs’.

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to date no ‘UFO’ report has
revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be
found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to
provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence
resources if we were to do so.

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters to the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

Finally, if you have information on a UFO sighting, and would like to inform us of it, please leave
the details on our UFO answerphone.

Thank you.

Yours sincerely

reg section 40)

Thank you for your letter dated 8 May concerning unidentified flying objects (UFOs) and your
comments regarding the release of a Ministry of Defence report entitled “Unidentified Aerial
Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region”. Your letter has been passed to this Department as
we are the focal point within the MOD for correspondence regarding UFOs (also known as UAP)
and the Department jointly responsible with the Defence Intelligence Staff (DIS) for the release of
this report.

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of
'UFO/flying saucer’ matters or to the question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial
lifeforms. We remain totally open-minded, but to date the MOD knows of no evidence which
substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena. The MOD examines any reports of
'UFOs' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence
significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace might
have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source we do not attempt to identify the
precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such
as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the
MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of
public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit

The MOD report was released in response to a Freedom of Information request from an academic
researcher. The MOD understands the public interest in information about UFOs and has
embraced the spirit of open government. All requests for information are answered as fully as
possible and, despite the size and original classification of this report, staff have taken great care
to ensure that the majority of this large report is made available. We are pleased to be able to
make the report available to a wide audience via the MOD website and if you have not yet had an
opportunity to see the full report, you may wish to view it at

http://www.mod.uk/Defencelnternet/FreedomOfinformation/PublicationScheme .

With regard to your comments about the content of the report, I can advise you that the study was
conducted purely to establish whether the UFO sighting reports received by the MOD were of any
value to the DIS and whether there was a requirement for the DIS to see them in the future. Given
the conclusion it was decided that there was no such requirement and, since December 2000, UFO
reports have not been forwarded to the DIS. We appreciate that there may be those who disagree
with the report’s findings, but the MOD is satisfied that its conclusions are sufficient for defence
needs.

As for the details you provided of UFO sightings in West Germany and Thailand, I should inform
you that the MOD is only responsible for the UK Air Policing Area and does not therefore get

involved with UFO reports in other countries. Defence of this airspace would, of course, be a
matter for the nations concerned.

Finally, you mentioned the well known reported sighting at Rendlesham Forest in December
1980. You may be interested to learn that all the papers the MOD holds on these events have also
been released and can be reviewed on the MOD website as the address given above.

Yours sincerely,

BBC NEWS | Technology | UK hacker ‘should be extradited’ Page 1 of 2

e GIES News

UK hacker ‘should be extradited'

Mr McKinnon could face a lengthy jail sentence in the US

McKinnon's reaction
UK hacker Gary McKinnon should be recommended for extradition to the US,
a district judge has ruled.

The decision means Mr McKinnon will face trial in America for what the US has called
"the biggest military hack of all time".

Although he has admitted hacking US military networks, Mr McKinnon said he was
motivated by curiosity not malice.

The final decision on whether he should be sent to the US for trial rests with the
home secretary.

Hack attack

The decision was given at Bow Street Magistrates' Court in London and ends three years of uncertainty
for Mr McKinnon.

Speaking outside the court after hearing the decision Mr McKinnon said: "It went as expected and now
the appeals process can now start."

Karen Todner, Mr McKinnon's solicitor, said: "We're obviously very disappointed with the judgement that
was given this morning. We're proposing to appeal this to the Secretary of State, and if we're still
refused we will then appeal to the High Court for a decision to allow Gary to be tried here as a British
citizen."

GARY MCKINNON SPEAKS

Watch the interview

Mr McKinnon was first arrested in 2002 by the UK's National Hi-Tech Crime Unit for hacking into a series
of computer networks used by the US Army, Navy, Air Force, and Department of Defense.

The US in its case for extradition said Mr McKinnon caused more than $700,000 (£375,235) of damage
while exploring the computer networks at various US military institutions.

It said one attack at the Earle Naval Weapons Station took place soon after September 11, 2001 made it
impossible to use critical systems. The US Department of Justice said it took a month to get systems
working in the aftermath of this attack.

Mr McKinnon has admitted that he spent almost two years exploring these networks but has said he was
motivated by a search for what he called "suppressed technology".

In a recent BBC interview, Mr McKinnon said he had got close to getting pictorial evidence of
technologies that could be of huge benefit to everyone but the US government was not releasing.

http://newsvote.bbc.co.uk/mpapps/pagetools/print/news.bbc.co.uk/1/hi/technology/4757375.stm 10/05/2006

BBC NEWS | Technology | UK hacker 'should be extradited! Page 2 of 2

In numerous interviews about the case, Mr McKinnon has resisted attempts to portray him as a hacking
mastermind. By contrast he said he was a "bumbling hacker" that exploited the jax security policies of
the US military.

Speaking after the hearing ended, he said: "My intention was never to disrupt security. The fact that I
logged on with no password showed there was no security to begin with."

Ms Todner said she was worried that any sentence Mr McKinnon received in the US would be
"disproportionate" to the scale of the offences he committed.

"There is power under the Computer Misuse Act to charge him here and he could stand trial here," said
Ms Todner. "In fact, had that happened he probably would have been tried, served his sentence and
have been released by now"

If tried and found guilty in the US, Mr McKinnon could face decades in jai! and fines totalling millions of
dollars.

The Prime Minister/SofS/Min(AF)/Min(DP)/USofS/MOD" has received the attached
correspondence from a member of the public, which this office has neither retained nor

acknowledged. Please send a reply on behalf of the PM/Minister/Department’.

Ministers attach great importance to correspondence being answered promptly, and your reply
should be sent within 15 working days of the above date. If, exceptionally, this should prove
impossible, an interim reply should be sent within the same timescale. You should be aware that
No 10 periodically calls for a sample of letters sent by officials on the PM's behalf for his
perusal.

Most correspondence involves some form of request for information — even if it is only a request
for clarification of Government policy — and is therefore coveréd by the Freedom of Information
Act (FOIA) from January 2005. In general, if you meet the deadline for responding to
correspondence, and comply with any requests for information, there is no need to do anything
differently as this will meet the requirements of the Act. However, if the correspondence
requests information which is not already in the public domain, and which might need to be
withheld, then you should treat it as a FOLA request, track it using the Access to Information
toolkit, and comply with the separate FOI guidance from DG Info (see
http://aitportal/default.aspx for details). However, the deadline for responding to correspondence —
will still apply. If you are in any doubt as to whether a piece of correspondence should be treated
as an FOIA request, you should ask your FOI Focal Point or refer to the guidance produced by
DG Info.

It is vital that branches ensure they have simple systems to record and track correspondence
received from members of the public. This information should be regularly monitored and
reviewed against the targets for answering correspondence published in the Spending Review .
2000 Service Delivery Agreement for the Ministry of Defence.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the accuracy of your branch
records on correspondence will be performed throughout the year.

Ministerial Correspondence Unit
Floor 5, Zone A, Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB

evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting.
We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural
phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide
this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of public
funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

With regard to your particular observations, we are satisfied that there is no
corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom’s airspace has been
breached by unauthorised aircraft.

If you wish to see information about UFOs which has been released to the public from
MOD records, please look at the MOD website
http://www.mod.uk/DefenceInternet/FreedomOfInformation/PublicationScheme
There is also information available for public viewing at The National Archives.
Details can be found on their website at http://www .nationalarchives.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely,

Ministry of Defence

received by the MOD were of any value to the DIS and whether there was a

I am writing concerning your email of 8 May regarding extraterrestrials and UFOs.
Your message has been passed to this department because we are the focal point
within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence about UFOs.

It might be helpful if I explain that the MOD does not have any expertise or role in
respect of 'UFO/flying saucer’ matters or to the question of the existence or otherwise
of extraterrestrial lifeforms. We remain totally open-minded but to date the MOD
knows of no evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.
Any reports of 'UFO' sightings the MOD receives are examined solely to establish
whether what was seen might have some defence significance; namely, whether there
is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace might have been compromised by
hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to
the United Kingdom from an external source, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed
such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each reported
sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or
natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the MOD to
provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of
public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

If you wish to see information the MOD has released about UFOs, please look at the
MOD website at http://www.mod.uk/Defencelnternet/FreedomOflnformation/

Yours sincerely,

in respect of 'UFO/flying saucer’ matters or to the question of the existence or

phenomena. The Ministry of Defence examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying
objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance, namely, whether there is any evidence that the United
Kingdom's airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air

sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or

Woolwich UFO Research Group

lam writing concerning your letter of gh May regarding the recent release of an MOD report
entitled “Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region”. You seem to have
misunderstood the purpose of this report and the circumstances surrounding its release, it may
therefore be helpful if I explain this in more detail.

As stated in previous correspondence, the Ministry of Defence has no expertise or role in respect
of 'UFO/flying saucer' matters or to the question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial
lifeforms. To date the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates the existence of these
alleged phenomena and is not involved in any search for such phenomena. Our only interest in
the UFO sighting reports which we receive is to establish whether they provide any evidence of
anything that may be of defence concern. Once it is established that a report does not provide such
evidence no further investigation is carried out by the MOD and we do not attempt to positively
identify what was seen. It is not the MOD’s role to provide an aerial identification service and
would not be an efficient use of resources.

With regard to this study and the subsequent report that has now been released, I can inform you
that this study was not conducted to search for, or investigate, the possibility of extraterrestrial
life-forms. Until 1996 in line with our policy of examining reports for anything of defence
significance, UFO sighting reports from either military or civilian sources were sent to the
Defence Intelligence Staff (DIS) in case they contained any information of value in DIS’s task of
analysing the performance and threat of foreign weapons systems, nuclear, chemical and
biological warfare programmes and technologies and emerging technologies. In 1996 the MOD
held a policy review concerning the handling of these reports and DIS decided to conduct a study
to establish once and for all whether they were of any value to their work. Based on all the
available evidence remaining in the Department (reported over a 30 year period) the study
concluded that UFO sighting reports did not have any significant Defence Intelligence value and
since then they have not been sent to DIS.

As for your comment about the length of time taken to conduct this study and Mr Pope’s
involvement, I can confirm that the study was conducted between December 1996 and March
2000. Mr Pope was not involved with the study. During an earlier policy review during Mr Pope’s
employment in this branch (previously know as Secretariat (Air Staff)) a similar study was
suggested by DIS, but was never conducted. Mr Pope left Secretariat (Air Staff) in 1994 some two
years before this study was started and six years before its completion.
With regard to the circumstances surrounding the release of this report, I can confirm that the
MOD received a Freedom of Information request for a copy of the report. The MOD has
embraced the spirit of open government and despite the size (over 460 pages) and original
classification (Secret), MOD staff have taken great care to ensure that the majority of this large
report is made available. Sections have been withheld only where the information is covered by
FOIA exemptions, and it is considered that it is not in the public interest to release it. If you wish
to view the entire report please look at the Freedom of Information page of the MOD website at
http://www.mod.uk/Defencelntrenet/FreedomOfinformation/PublicationScheme. An explanation
of the reasons for withholding a smal! amount of information from the report can be found in the
covering letter to the individual who requested the por cr in the Disclosure Log
on the website.

You also asked how much this project was budgeted for annually. There was no annual budget for
conducting this study. The study and subsequent report were conducted by one person who was
initially employed as a contractor and commenced work on the report in December 1996: He was
employed on a part-time basis to work with the DIS (not solely on this project) and divided his
time between working for the DIS and working on his company’s business. He left his company
on early retirement in ES following which he was employed as a consultant for the DIS
until completion of the report in March 2000. During the period December 1996 to March 2000,
producing the report was only one of several tasks in which he was engaged and it is, therefore,
not possible to determine how much of his time was divided between producing the report and his
other tasks.

In your letter you also comment about being “stopped many times on public land by the military
and what were presumed to be other Security Services” when you have been on Skywatches.

I am not aware of any such incidents or indeed why this would be considered necessary, unless of
course, you were on MOD land or in the immediate vicinity of a military establishment.

Finally, you mention dis-information across the media concerning UFO research and inaccuracies
in Ms Georgina Bruni’s book ‘You Can’t Tell The People’. The MOD makes official information
available whenever possible in accordance with the Freedom of Information Act 2000 and when
we believe requested information may of interest to a wider audience, it is placed on the MOD
website. We are not responsible for how the media report UFO matters or what is written by
individual authors.

Yours sincerely,

I am writing to you concerning the disclosure of information relating to project
Over the past 25 years I have had many replies from departments in the Ministry of
Defence stating they have made no secret investigations into the studies of UFO’s (or
E.T. craft) and have only a limited interest in UFO sightings.

Then J hear on Sunday that the Ministry of Defence has been conducting a secret 4

3. A majority of UFO reports are tenuous in nature, are reported
at second hand or with a time lag, and frequently overland or at
night in areas where we have little radar cover. Those described
as at great height, if they exist, may lie above radar cover, as
only Fylingdales has tracking capabi ities in the endo-atmosphere
and in space. Some reports describe objects in terms of
Manoeuvre, speed and shape which lie beyond our engineering

4. There is considerable difficulty, therefore, in assessing and
prioritising these reports sufficiently quickly to Provide, where _
warranted, an active response. Moreover, when interceptions may
be needed, we are constrained by reduced readiness following
drawdowns at the end of the Cold War and the considerable time lag
in responding from northern bases in the event of incidents in the
south, especially if access is needed to intensively used civil

1am writing with reference to your e.mail regarding ‘unidentified flying objects’ that you
passed to us on the 26 April 2006. This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence
for correspondence relating to UFOs. Sorry for the long delay in getting back to you.

First, | am not disputing that people see objects that they can not identify, but it may be
helpful if | explain that the MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying
saucer’ matters to the question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms,
about which it remains totally open-minded. | should add that to date, the MOD knows of no
evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged phenomena.

Itis clearly outside the Department’s remit to devote resources to determining the precise
identity of every seemingly inexplicable sight in the sky. It is quite normal for a sighting to
remain unexplained, but require no further official action. (Some of the reports we receive
could be hoaxes, and others could be put down to things such as laser light shows, spot lights,
satellites, meteorites, weather balloons and so forth). The MOD does not seek to provide a
precise explanation for the reports we receive each year, unless there is corroborating
evidence to suggest that the Uk’s airspace may have been compromised by unauthorised
aircraft. Once content there is no evidence of a defence concern, the reports are then placed
on file. ‘

Sorry | could not be more helpful.

The Prime Minister/SofS/Min(AF)/Min(DPVUSofS/MOD" has received the attached
correspondence from a member of the public, which this office has neither retained nor
acknowledged. Please send a reply on behalf of the PM/Minister/Department'’.

Ministers attach great importance to correspondence being answered promptly, and your reply
should be sent within 15 working days of the above date. If, exceptionally, this should prove

. impossible, an interim reply should be sent within the same timescale. You should be aware that
No 10 periodically calls for a sample of letters sent by officials on the PM's behalf for his

perusal.

Most correspondence involves some form of request for information — even if it is only a request
for clarification of Government policy — and is therefore covered by the Freedom of Information
Act (FOIA) from January 2005. In general, if you meet the deadline for responding to
correspondence, and comply with any requests for information, there is no:need to do anything
differently as-this will meet the requirements of the Act. However, if the correspondence
requests information which is not already in the public domain, and which might need to be
withheld, then you should treat it as a FOIA request, track it using the Access to Information
toolkit, and comply with the separate FOI guidance from DG Info (see :
http:// aitportal/default.aspx for details). However, the deadline for responding to correspondence
will still apply. If you are in any doubt as to whether a piece of correspondence should be treated
as an FOIA request, you should ask your FOI Focal Point or refer to the guidance produced by
-DG Info.

It is vital that branches ensure they have simple systems to record and track correspondence
received from members of the public. This information should be regularly monitored and
reviewed against the targets for answering correspondence published in the Spending Review
2000 Service Delivery Agreement for the Ministry of Defence.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the accuracy of your branch
records on correspondence will be performed throughout the year.

Subject: Internet-authorised:Unexplained Lights

Thank you for your email dated 26 April in reply to mine, concerning your UFO sighting. ! will now address the various
points raised in your latest message.

First with regard to your comment about the MOD “covering up UFO activity around the country”, | can assure you that
the MOD has been receiving reported UFO sightings and corresponding with the public about UFO matters since the
1950s and has never denied that we receive such reports or refused to give details of our limited interest in such matters.

As for the low flying helicopter training you experienced, it is possible that these helicopters were taking part in an
exercise in your area, which involved an element of low flying, however without a specific date | am unable to provide any
further details of this training. | can however, assure you that this activity was not conducted in response to any reported
UFO sightings.

You also asked “why have so many UFO documents been covered-up in the past and marked Top Secret and higher?” ”.
| am aware that many people believe that documents the MOD holds on UFO matters are highly classified, but this is
simply not true. No documents are marked Top Secret, in fact the vast majority are unclassified.

With regard to your comment that “you don’t seem to want to release documents until they have to be released into the
public domain under the Freedom of Information Act’. Again, this is not the case. Prior to the introduction of the Freedom
of Information 2000 (FOIA) on 1 January 2005, ail government files were subject to the provisions of the Public Records
Act of 1958 and 1967. This Act of Parliament stated that official files generally remain closed from public viewing for 30
years after the last action has been taken. It was the case that before 1967 “UFO" files were destroyed after five years,
as there was insufficient public interest in the subject to merit their permanent retention. However since 1967, following an
increase in public interest in this subject "UFO" report files have been routinely preserved. Any files from the 1950s and
early 1960s which did survive and files dating from 1967 to 1979 are available for examination by members of the public
at The National Archives, Ruskin Avenue, Kew, Richmond, Surrey, TW9 4DU. Files are added to this collection when
they have completed the MOD internal review processes. If you wish to see what files are available and details of how to
view them, please look at The National Archives website at www.nationalarchives.gov.uk. In addition to this information
the MOD has released information (prior to the introduction of the FOIA) in accordance with the Code of Practice on
Access to Government Information (the Code). This information together with further releases under the FOIA can be
viewed on the Freedom of Information pages of the MOD website at www.mod.uk. Further information is added to this
site whenever possible.

As for your comments about the Rendlesham Forest incident and alleged threats made to individuals involved. | can
assure you that no such tactics are ever used to prevent the release of information about UFO sightings. Sensitive
information about any subject, will be withheld in accordance with appropriate sections of the FOIA. All of the documents
the MOD holds concerning this incident were released into the public domain several years ago and can now be viewed
on the MOD website.

Finally, thank you for the photographs attached to your message. While we are always interested to see photographs the
public wish to send to us, as explained in my previous message we do not attempt to positively identify each sighting
reported to us. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom’s airspace
was breached by unauthorised aircraft.

| hope this is helpful.

Ministry of Defence

@: am writing in response to your e-mail of 23 March concerning lights
seen near your home over the past three years. Your message has been
passed to this department as we are the focal point within the
Ministry of Defence for correspondence about Unidentified Flying
Objects. I apologise for the delay in responding.

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence
does not have any expertise or role in respect of 'UFO/flying saucer'
matters or to the question of the existence or otherwise of
extraterrestrial life-forms. We remain totally open-minded, but to
date we know of no evidence which substantiates the existence of these alleged
henomena.
The Ministry of Defence examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying
objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might
have some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence
that the United Kingdom's airspace might have been compromised by
hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to
date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to
identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it
is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or
natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function
of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We
could not justify expenditure of public funds on investigations which
go beyond our specific defence remit.

I can assure you that the integrity of the UK's airspace in peacetime
is maintained through continuous surveillance of the UK Air Policing
Area by the Royal Air Force. This is achieved by using a combination
of civil and military radar installations, which provide a continuous
real-time "picture" of the UK airspace. Any threat to the UK Air
Policing Area would be handled in the light of the particular
circumstances at the time (it might if deemed appropriate, involve the
scrambling or diversion of air defence aircraft). From that
perspective, reports provided to us of 'UFO' sightings are examined,
but consultation with air defence staff and others as necessary is
considered only where there is sufficient evidence to suggest a breach
of UK air space. The vast majority of reports we receive are very
sketchy and vague. Only a handful of reports in recent years have
warranted further investigation and none revealed any evidence of a threat.

With regard to your comments concerning the presents of low flying
military helicopters in the vicinity shortly after you saw an orange
light, I should inform you that low flying training takes place
throughout the UK and is not connected to UFO sightings. One of the
main roles of military helicopters is to provide close support to
ground forces. In the event of conflict helicopters would have to fly
at very low levels to reinforce troops in the field undetected by
highly developed radar systems. Low level training for helicopters
generally takes place at heights below 500 feet down to ground level.
Helicopters are vulnerable to ground fire and one of the vital skills
which must be acquired by the pilots is flying as closely as possible
to the nap of the earth so that the aircraft is shielded and
camouflaged by the features of the terrain. This is a perishable skill
that needs to be practiced regularly. This type of training is
therefore spread as thinly as possible throughout the UK so as not to
concentrate activity over one are and to keep disturbance to the
public to a minimum. I am sorry if this training has caused disturbance to you.

Yours sincerely,

Defence examines any reports of ‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish
whether what was seen might have some defence significance; namely, whether there is any
evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace might have been compromised by hostile or
unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom
from an external source, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, we do not
attempt to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that
rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them,
but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We
could not justify expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific
defence remit.

I can assure you that the integrity of the UK's airspace in peacetime is maintained through
continuous surveillance of the UK Air Policing Area by the Royal Air Force. This is achieved
by using a combination of civil and military radar installations, which provide a continuous
real-time “picture” of the UK airspace. Any threat to the UK Air Policing Area would be
handled in the light of the particular circumstances at the time (it might if deemed appropriate,
involve the scrambling or diversion of air defence aircraft). From that perspective, reports
provided to us of ‘UFO’ sightings are examined, but consultation with air defence staff and
others as necessary is considered only where there is sufficient evidence to suggest a breach
of UK air space. The vast majority of reports we receive are very sketchy and vague. Only a
handful of reports in recent years have warranted further investigation and none revealed any
evidence of a threat.

With regard to your comments concerning the presents of low flying military helicopters in the
vicinity shortly after you saw an orange light, | should inform you that low flying training takes
place throughout the UK and is not connected to UFO sightings. One of the main roles of
military helicopters is to provide close support to ground forces. In the event of conflict
helicopters would have to fly at very low levels to reinforce troops in the field undetected by
highly developed radar systems. Low level training for helicopters generally takes place at
heights below 500 feet down to ground level. Helicopters are vulnerable to ground fire and
one of the vital skills which must be acquired by the pilots is flying as closely as possible to
the nap of the earth so that the aircraft is shielded and camouflaged by the features of the
terrain. This is a perishable skill that needs to be practiced regularly. This type of training is
therefore spread as thinly as possible throughout the UK so as not to concentrate activity over
one are and to keep disturbance to the public to a minimum. | am sorry if this training has
caused disturbance to you.

Yours sincerely,

Ministry of Defence

Thank you for your letter of 8" February concerning records of UFO sightings. Your letter has
been passed to this Department, as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence about UFOs.

I can confirm that there is no underground bunker beneath Whitehall that contains records of UFO
sightings. This Department holds records of UFO sightings reported to the MOD and
correspondence with the public about UFO related matters covering a 25 year period. These are
held on paper files, the vast majority of which are stored in archives in Derbyshire, with a small
number of the later files held within this Department.

Some of the information from our records has been released into the public domain and we intend
to continue releasing information in the future. If you would like to view the material that has
been released please look at the Freedom of Information Disclosure Log on the MOD website at
www.mod.uk. Records from the early 1950s and up to 1978 are open for viewing at The National
Archives, Kew, Richmond, Surrey. Details of the records available and how to access them can be
found on The National Archives website at www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

lam writing concerning your last two messages left on Das answerphone.

As stated in my previous letters to you, we can not help you with the various problems you are
experiencing. These are not issues that this Department of the Ministry of Defence deals with or
can help you with, obviously we would if we could. You need to pursue other avenues that could
help you.

We do not investigate UFO sightings, ie. the lights you saw in the sky. It is clearly outside the
Department’s remit to devote resources to determining the precise identity of every seemingly
inexplicable sight in the sky. It is quite normal for a sight to remain unexplained but require no
further official action. The MOD does not seek to provide a precise explanation for the reports we
receive each year, unless there is corroborating evidence to suggest that the UK’s airspace may
have been compromised by unauthorised aircraft. Once content there is no evidence of a defence
concern, the reports are placed on file. However, we believe that rational explanations could be
found for most of the sightings if resources were devoted to doing so.

Finally, as mentioned before in my letter of 11 January 2006, the problems you are experiencing
could be a matter for the civil police. You should seek help from them and they may be able to
help you.

Iam writing concerning your letter dated 23 December 2005. Sorry for the long delay in getting

I can assure you that the integrity of the UK’s airspace in peacetime is maintained through
continuous surveillance of the UK Air Policing Area by the Royal Air Force. This is achieved by
