e UFO SIGHTING
On Friday, 2"? September 2005, my Cousin and I both saw an Unidentified Flying Object.
Place: Rhossili Bay, South Wales.
Time: Approx 21.30.

Witnesses: :
Rees cCagueerSection 40 | currently engaged in writing and

translating books on military, naval and aviation history.

Circumstances:

My Cousin and I had walked my Portuguese Mountain Dog on the beach, and after the sun set we
slowly made our way back to the cliff path and began to climb it with the aid of a torch.

Halfway up the steep path we sat down on a bench and looked up at the clear night sky. It was a
cloudless night, with no moon. The stars were clearly visible since we were about ten miles from
the lights of Swansea, the nearest major conglomeration.

An airliner flew over, then what I took to be a helicopter, both showing flashing lights.

Next we saw what appeared to be a star which moved, from West to East. This I assumed to be a
satellite. When this satellite reached the shadow of the edge of the Earth, it became invisible.

My Cousin and I leaned back, looking vertically upwards, discussing the stars spread before us.
Suddenly we became aware of a bright object, travelling at very high speed, moving horizontally
from West to East almost directly above our heads.

Description:

The object was shaped like a pair of stubby swept-back wings, meeting at around 90 degrees. The
wingtips were round, as was the forward joint of port and starboard wings.

There was no visible fuselage or tail or engines.

The whole craft was white, matt rather than shiny, and reminded me afterwards of parian marble.
It made no sound, but then we had heard no sound either from the aircraft we had noticed earlier.
It displayed no navigation lights.

After what must have been less than a full second, the object disappeared.

My cousin turned to me in surprise, and J exclaimed “What on earth was that thing we just saw?”.
He suggested it was a shooting star. This thought had also passed through my mind as my brain
tried to rationalise what we had just seen. | replied that shooting stars do not have wings, and they
usually follow a parabolic path downwards, whereas this winged object was flying straight and
level.

We had no means of guesstimating its size, speed or height.

Puzzled as to why it was clearly visible for a brief time, then became invisible, I have since
surmised that, like the satellite we had previously observed, this object was illuminated by the rays
of the sun below the horizon. When it flew into the Earth’s shadow it ceased to be visible.
However, the angle involved at the moment it ceased to be illuminated, based on my observation
of the satellite, would mean that the flying object would have passed over at a great height. This
would then mean it was travelling at extremely high speed. Equally, to see an object which
appeared some two inches across from such a great height would mean it was of considerable size.
aA

spiritual war going on. That we do not understand what he is trying to explain to us.

the MOD does not have the knowledge/expertise of the phenomena that he is
speaking of. He has rang Das answerphone a number of times now, and just says
exactly the same thing.

No further action taken.

I am writing concerning your message left on the Das answerphone on 8 November 2005.

This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to UFOs.
Our answerphone is not a general helpline. It is for members of the public to leave reports of UFO
sightings. We do not phone members of the public to discuss UFO’s or any other issues they may
have. This office corresponds by letter or e.mail.

Thank you for your e-mail concerning reports of UFO sightings. | have also been given the
message you sent to public @ministers.mod.uk on 11" November.

| can confirm that this department is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence regarding UFO matters in the UK. !t may be helpful to you if | explain the
role of the Ministry of Defence regarding these matters.

The Ministry of Defence does not have any expertise or role in respect of 'UFO/flying saucer’
matters or to the question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial life forms. Although
we remain totally open-minded, to date the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates
the existence of these alleged phenomena. We do not investigate UFO sightings, but
examine any reports we receive solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's
airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is
evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, (and to date no
‘UFO' report has revealed such evidence), we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft
lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the MOD to
provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of public
funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

| hope this is helpful.

The Prime Minister/SofS/Min(AF)/Min(DP)/USofS/MOD* has received the attached
correspondence from a member of the public, which this office has neither retained nor
acknowledged. Please send a reply on behalf of the PM/Minister/Department’.

Ministers attach great importance to correspondence being answered promptly, and your reply
should be sent within 15 working days of the above date. If, exceptionally, this should prove
impossible, an interim reply should be sent within the same timescale. You should be aware that
No 10 periodically calls for a sample of letters sent by officials on the PM's behalf for his
perusal. .

Most correspondence involves some form of request for information — even if it is only a request
for clarification of Government policy — and is therefore covered by the Freedom of Information
Act (FOIA) from January 2005. In general, if you meet the deadline for responding to
‘correspondence, and comply with any requests for information, there is no need to do anything
differently as this will meet the requirements of the Act. However, if the correspondence
requests information which is not already in the public domain, and which might need to be ~
withheld, then you should treat it as a FOIA request, track it using the Access to Information
toolkit, and comply with the separate FOI guidance from DG Info (see
http://aitportal/default.aspx for details). However, the deadline for responding to correspondence
will still apply. If you are in any doubt as to whether a piece of correspondence should be treated

as an FOIA request, you should ask your FOI Focal Point or refer to the guidance produced by
DG Info.

It is vital that branches ensure they have simple systems to record and track correspondence
received from members of the public. This information should be regularly monitored and
reviewed against the targets for answering correspondence published in the Spending Review
2000 Service Delivery Agreement for the Ministry of Defence.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the accuracy of your branch
records on correspondence will be performed throughout the year.

section 40 |

I have received your message you left on our answerphone. As stated in my previous letter of 10
November 2005, we have a limited interest in the subject of UFOs.

I am not disputing that you had sightings that you could not identify on the 5/6/8 November 2005,
but we did not have any other members of the public inform us of any sightings on those dates
from Gloucestershire. A lot of reports are made to local newspapers, police stations, RAF stations
and air traffic control centres, but we do not always get informed of these. Sometimes a member
of the public sends us newspaper clippings or informs us of a website to look at as you have done.
Some of the reports we receive could be hoaxes, and others could be put down to things such as
laser light shows, spot lights, satellites, meteorites, weather balloons etc.

The MOD does not study or research the phenomena of UFOs, and this office, which forms part of
an RAF Secretariat Branch, is not constituted as a ‘UFO’ information bureau. We look at reports
as part of our normal duties to seek to establish whether a sighting might represent something
which is of defence concern.

It is clearly outside the Department’s defence remit to devote resources to determining the precise
identity of every seemingly inexplicable sight in the sky. It is quite normal for a sighting to remain
unexplained but require no further official action. Once content that there is no evidence of a
matter of defence concern, the reports are placed on file.

Finally, the integrity of the UK’s airspace in peacetime is maintained through continuous
surveillance of the UK Air Policing Area by the Royal Air Force. This is achieved by using a
combination of civil and military radar installations, which provide a continuous real-time
‘picture’ of the UK airspace. So if we became aware of evidence which might suggest a potential
threat, action would be set in hand to investigate, analyse and counter that threat, in the light of the
circumstances that prevail at the time. This applies to any form of threat to the UK’s security from
whatever source. I should point out that to date, the MOD is not aware of any evidence which
might substantiate the existence of craft or life forms of extraterrestrial origin, and no threat has
been discerned which has been attributed to a ‘UFO’.
I hope that this answers your question of the Ministry of Defence’s role in dealing with the subject
of UFOs.

Yours sincerely

section <0

I am writing with reference to your message left on our answerphone on the 11 October 2005.

On the answerphone you requested a number for this section. We have the answerphone for
members of the public to leave messages on, to do with the subject of UFOs. We do not give out
our personal work numbers from this Department to members of the public, we correspond by
letter or e.mail.

You asked about the issue of our airspace, (national security) being compromised and how the
Ministry of Defence would deal with the situation. Let me assure you the integrity of the UK’s
airspace in peacetime is maintained through continuous surveillance of the UK Air Policing Area
by the Royal Air Force. This is achieved by using a combination of civil and military radar
installations, which provide a continuous real-time “picture” of the UK airspace. Any threat to the
UK Air Defence Region would be handled in the light of the particular circumstances at the time
(it might, if deemed appropriate, involve the scrambling or diversion of air defence aircraft). (This
would apply to any form of threat to the UK’s security from whatever source). From that
perspective, reports provided to us of ‘UFO’ sightings are examined, but consultation with air
defence staff and others as necessary is considered only where there is sufficient evidence to
suggest a breach of UK air space. The vast majority of reports we receive are very sketchy and
vague. Only a handful of reports in recent years have warranted investigation and none revealed
any evidence of a threat. I should add, the Ministry of Defence does not rely on members of the
public informing us of aerial sightings in order to defend UK airspace.

Finally, I should point out that to date, the MOD is not aware of any evidence which might
substantiate the existence of craft or lifeforms of extraterrestrial origin, and no threat has been
discerned which has been attributed to a ‘UFO’.

I hope this letter has answered your questions and I have nothing further to add.

Yours sincerely

others as necessary is considered only where there is sufficient evidence to suggest a breach
of UK air space. The vast majority of reports we receive are very sketchy and vague. Only a
handful of reports in recent years have warranted investigation and none revealed any
evidence of a threat.

i should point out that to date, the MOD is not aware of any evidence which might
substantiate the existence of craft or lifeforms of extraterrestrial origin, and no threat has been
discerned which has been attributed to a ‘UFO’.

Some of the reports we receive could be hoaxes, and others could be put down to things such
as laser light shows, spot lights, satellites, meteorites, weather balloons etc.

As to your question of the remit to investigate aerial phenomena, no department in the
Ministry of Defence has the funds to carry out these investigations. As mentioned before in
my previous letters, the MOD has a limited interest in the subject of ‘UFO's. The Ministry of
Defence does not rely on people to inform us of sightings for defence reasons, we already
have that strategy in place.

| hope this e.mail has answered your questions.

Yours sincerely

Ministers attach great importance to correspondence being answered promptly, and your reply
should be sent within 15 working days of the above date. If, exceptionally, this should prove
impossible, an interim reply should be sent within the same timescale. You should be aware that
No 10 periodically calls for a sample of letters sent by officials on the PM's behalf for his
perusal.

Most correspondence involves some form of request for information — even if it is only a request
for clarification of Government policy — and is therefore covered by the Freedom of Information
Act (FOIA) from January 2005. In general, if you meet the deadline for responding to
correspondence, and comply with any requests for information, there is no need to do anything
differently as this will meet the requirements of the Act. However, if the correspondence
requests information which is not already in the public domain, and which might need to be
withheld, then you should treat it as a FOIA request, track it using the Access to Information
toolkit, and comply with the separate FOI guidance from DG Info (see
http://aitportal/default.aspx for details). However, the deadline for responding to correspondence
will still apply. If you are in any doubt as to whether a piece of correspondence should be treated
as an FOIA request, you should.ask your FOI Focal Point or refer to the guidance produced by
DG Info.

It is vital that branches ensure they have simple systems to record and track correspondence
received from members of the public. This information should be regularly monitored and
reviewed against the targets for answering correspondence published in the Spending Review
2000 Service Delivery Agreement for the Ministry of Defence.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the accuracy of your branch
records on correspondence will be performed throughout the year.

I am writing with reference to your e.mail that you sent to the Public Ministers Office on 30 June
2005. The e.mail has been passed to me and I have been asked to reply. Sorry for the delay in
getting back to you.

This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to
‘UFOs’.

As mentioned before in my previous letter dated 17 March 2005, the MOD has a limited interest
in UFOs. We have no expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters to the question of
the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which it remains totally open-
minded. I should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena. Hence, we know not of any ET visitations.

You may wish to know that there is already some information about UFOs available for viewing.
MOD files were routinely destroyed after 5 years until 1967 when they were generally preserved
for The National Archives. A few have survived before 1967 and these with records up to 1977
are now available for viewing. The National Archives can be contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Kew,
Richmond, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone, 020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a
website giving information about the records they hold and how to access them. This can be found
on the internet at http://www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

As to your question of the ‘Rendlesham Forest’ incident, you may wish to know that all the
surviving MOD documents relating to the Rendlesham Forest incident have been released into the
MOD Freedom of Information Publication Scheme and can be viewed on the internet at
www.foi.mod.uk. A search under ‘Rendlesham Forest’ will take you directly to these documents.

Hope this will be of help.

Yours sincerely

a

I am writing concerning your three undated letters sent to the Ministry of Defence concerning
your experiences in 1981 and unidentified flying objects seen over your home while you were
living in New Zealand. Your letters have been passed to me, as this Department is the focal point
within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence about UFOs.

You may recall, I wrote to you on 29 March 2004 in response to your previous letter about these
matters. In that letter I explained Ministry of Defence policy regarding UFO sightings, namely
that we examine the UFO sightings we receive solely to establish whether there is any evidence
that UK airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless
there is such evidence we do not attempt to identify what has been seen. However, as your
sightings took place in New Zealand’s airspace (not the UK), this is clearly a matter for the New
Zealand authorities and you may wish to contact them.

With regard to the film you were shown in 1981 showing you your future, I know of no such
activities carried out by MOD officials or for what defence purpose these might occur. Iam
therefore unable to assist you with your enquiries into these events.

Yours sincerely,

Pp

© .. gam v

lam writing concerning your message of 31 July concerning the address by which you can
report UFO sightings. Your letter has been passed to this department as we are the focal
point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence regarding UFOs.

First it may be helpful if | explain that the MOD does not have any role or expertise with
respect to flying saucers or extraterrestrial lifeforms. While we remain

open minded, to date we know of no evidence as to the existence of such phenomena. Any
UFO sightings reported to the MOD are examined solely to determine whether there is any
evidence that the UK air defence area has been compromised by unauthorised air activity.
Unless there is such evidence, we do not attempt to identify what might have been seen. It is
not the role of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service and we could not
justify public expenditure on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

Baring this in mind, if you wish to inform the MOD about a sighting you can contact us via any
of the following:

Ministry of Defence

Directorate of Air Staff - Freedom of Information
Level 5, Zone H

Main Building

Whitehall

London

SW1A 2HB

E-mail: das-ifo-office @mod.uk
Telephone:020 7218 2140 (24 hour answerphone)
| hope this is helpful.

wregocction 40

Thank you for your letter of 17°" July concerning two silver objects seen by a couple from
Livingston, over the Pentland Hills on 10" May 2005.

You asked whether there was any military activity in the area at the time of the sighting. First it
might be helpful if I explain that Livingston is within airspace controlled by Edinburgh Airport
Air Traffic Control and military aircraft do not train close to civilian airports. The couple who
observed this are therefore unlikely to see military aircraft close to their home. With regard to the
Pentland Hills, these are outside of this controlled airspace and it is possible to see military
aircraft training in this area.

With regard to this particular occasion, I should inform you that military aircrew do not use fixed
flight paths, but plan their sorties individually in order to practice a variety of scenarios. Details of
these sorties are not held centrally but kept by each Squadron and it is impossible to be certain
whether there was any aircraft in the area without asking every Squadron to check their aircrew
records. However, there is an exception to this, when low flying training is being conducted and
aircrew are required to make a booking with a central booking cell. Low flying training for fixed
wing aircraft is below 2000 feet and for helicopters, below 500 feet. I have checked these records
and there were no military aircraft low flying at the time of this sighting. I have also checked our
records for any military exercises in the area at the time and I can inform you that there was a
medium-scale, multi-national training exercise taking place from the 10" to the 12™ May
involving fast jet, Air to Air Refuelling and E-3 aircraft. However, as this exercise took place
between 0930 and 1430 daily it is unlikely to have been responsible for this observation.

I am sorry that I can not be more helpful.

Yours sincerely,

>

I am writing in response to your e.mail of 1 August 2005.

The MOD has a limited interest in the subject of UFOs, so Section 40 Fea will not be
investigated into. This focal point exists mainly for defence reasons as explained in my two letters
~ dated 20 January 2005 and 28 July 2005. It is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind
of aerial identification service.

The MOD does not study or research the phenomena of UFOs, and this office, which forms part of
an RAF Secretariat Branch, is not constituted as a ‘UFO’ information bureau. We look at reports
as part of our normal duties to seek to establish whether a sighting might represent something
which is of defence concern.

As it is clearly outside the Department’s defence remit to devote resources to determining the
precise identity of every seemingly inexplicable sight in the sky it is quite normal for a sighting to
remain unexplained but not require further official action. Once content that there is no evidence
of a matter of defence concern, the reports are placed on file.

Unless there is corroborating evidence to suggest that the UK’s airspace may have been
compromised by hostile or unauthorised aircraft, the MOD does not investigate or seek to provide
a precise explanation for each of the 200-300 ‘UFO’ reports we receive each year. However, we
believe that rational explanations could be found for most sightings if resources were devoted to
doing so.

Lhope that this answers your question of the Ministry of Defence’s role in dealing with the subject
of UFOs.

Yours sincerely

The Prime Minister/SofS/Min(AF)/Min(DP)/USofS/MOD* has received the attached
correspondence from a member of the public, which this office has neither retained nor
acknowledged. Please send a reply on behalf of the PM/Minister/Department”.

Ministers attach great importance to correspondence being answered promptly, and your reply
should be sent within 15 working days of the above date. If, exceptionally, this should prove
impossible, an interim reply should be sent within the same timescale. You should be aware that
No 10-periodically calls for a sample of tetters sent by officials on thé PM's behaif for his
perusal.

Most correspondence involves some form of request for information — even if it is only a request
for clarification of Government policy — and is therefore covered by the Freedom of Information
Act (FOIA) from January 2005. In general, if you meet the deadline for responding to
correspondence, and comply with any requests for information, there is no need to do anything
differently as this will meet the requirements of the Act. However, if the correspondence
requests information which is not already in the public domain, and which might need to be
withheld, then you should treat it as a FOIA request, track it using the Access to Information
toolkit, and comply with the separate FOI guidance from DG Info (see
http://aitportal/default.aspx for details). However, the deadline for responding to correspondence
will still apply. If you are in any doubt as to whether a piece of correspondence should be treated

as an FOIA request, you should ask your FOI Focal Point or refer to the guidance produced by
DG Info.

It is vital that branches ensure they have simple systems to record and track correspondence
received from members of the public. This information should be regularly monitored and
reviewed against the targets for answering correspondence published in the Spending Review
2000 Service Delivery Agreement for the Ministry of Defence.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the accuracy of your branch
records on correspondence will be performed throughout the year.

Ministerial Correspondence Unit

Floor 5, Zone tio Main _ —_— SW1A 2HB

SESE Du: Ministerial Correspondence; e: Ministerial-Correspondence@mod.uk.

Detailed guidance on handling TO Correspondence can be found on the Defence Intranet at hitp://main.defence. mod.uk/min_parl/ParlBrch/TOGuid.him

If you do not have access to the Intranet, please inform the Ministerial Correspondence Unit.

28 July 2005

Dear EE

I am writing in response to your e.mail of 14 July 2005, that you sent to this office.

I am sorry if my letter dated 20 January 2005 to SEROMA sounded dismissive, but as stated in
that letter we do have a limited interest in UFOs BERRI did not leave a date, time or details
of her sighting on the answerphone that was required.

We do keep an open mind about the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial activity, but the
general ‘UFO’ debate falls outside our specific remit relating to defence matters — we keep an
open mind and remain vigilant for anything which might indicate that the integrity of the UK’s Air
Defence Region has been compromised (which is why the focal point exists).

If we become aware of evidence which might suggest a potential threat, action would be set in
hand to investigate, analyse and counter that threat, in the light of the circumstances which prevail
at the time. This applies to any form of threat to the UK’s security from whatever source. I should
point out that to date, the MOD is not aware of any evidence which might substantiate the
existence of craft or lifeforms of extraterrestrial origin, and no threat has been discerned which has
been attributed to a ‘UFO’.

Also, as requested, the report form that we use is enclosed for your information.

Hope this will be helpful.

Yours sincerely

London Our Reference:
pene | Date
Date:
6 July 2005
Dear STS

I am writing with reference to your message regarding UFOs that you left on our answerphone.
This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to
‘UFOs.’

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and
to date no ‘UFO’ report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise
nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights
or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it
is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an
inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

The MOD does not have any expertise or role in respect of ‘UFO/flying saucer’ matters to the
question of the existence or otherwise of extraterrestrial lifeforms, about which it remains totally
open-minded. I should add that to date, the MOD knows of no evidence which substantiates the
existence of these alleged phenomena.

Reports from members of the public of sightings are usually made to Police stations, RAF stations
and air traffic control centres and are then forwarded to this office. Members of the public also
telephone the Das answerphone directly to report a sighting. The reports which are usually very
brief and vague, are considered, as necessary, in consultation with air defence advisers within the
MOD, and a decision is taken as to whether what was seen represents a threat to the security of the
UK. Sightings reports are kept on file within this office for future reference.

Hope this is helpful.
Yours sincerely

Ministers attach great importance to correspondence being answered promptly, and your reply
should be sent within 15 working days of the above date. If, exceptionally, this should prove
impossible, an interim reply should be sent within the same timescale.. You should be aware that
No 10 periodically calls for a sample of letters sent by officials on the PM's behalf for his
perusal.

Most correspondence involves some form of request for information — even if it is only a request
for clarification of Government policy — and is therefore covered by the Freedom of Information
Act (FOIA) from January 2005. In general, if you meet the deadline for responding to
correspondence, and comply with any requests for information, there is no need to do anything
differently as this will meet the requirements of the Act. However, if the correspondence
requests information which is not already in the public domain, and which might need to be
withheld, then you should treat it as a FOIA request, track it using the Access to Information
toolkit, and comply with the separate FOI guidance from DG Info (see
http://aitportal/default.aspx for details). However, the deadline for responding to correspondence
will still apply. If you are in any doubt as to whether a piece of correspondence should be treated
as an FOIA request, you should ask your FOI Focal Point or refer to the guidance produced by
DG Info.

It is vital that branches ensure they have simple systems to record and track correspondence
received from members of the public. This information should be regularly. monitored and
reviewed against the targets for answering correspondence published in the Spending Review
2000 Service Delivery Agreement for the Ministry of Defence.

As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on the accuracy of your branch
records on correspondence will be performed throughout the year.

Ministerial Correspondence Unit
Floor 5, Zone A, Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB

Section 40, DI: Ministerial Correspondence; e: Ministerial-Correspondence@mod.uk.

Detailed guidance on handling TO Correspondence can be found on the Defence Intranet at hitp://main.defence.mod.uk/min_parl/ParlBrch/TOGuid.htm

If you do not have access to the Intranet, please inform the Ministerial Correspondence Unit.

reap oection 40

Thank you for your letter dated 11 April 2005.

This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to
‘UFOs.’

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.

Unless there is evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and
to date no ‘UFO’ report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise
nature of each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights
or natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it
is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an
inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

With regards to the vapour trails you saw, civilian aircraft Of light aircraft could have been in the
area at that time, leaving the trails behind. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence
to suggest the United Kingdom’s airspace was breached by unauthorised aircraft.

As to the other issues you mention in your letter, this office does not have any responsibility for
those various matters and thus can’t comment on them.

Sorry I could not have been more help.

Yours sincerely
